## 1. Executive summary and status

Ten of ten major business domains have first-pass owner direction (BIZ §27.5). This is NOT a technical completion percentage. Media is split into broadcast and podcasts below for traceability, not a proposed additional domain. The task is R-2, not another broad inventory. The earlier register's recommended R-0 next step is superseded as a work-sequence instruction by the supplied Foundation Reconciliation Programme §7; unfinished R-0/R-1 obligations remain open. The 19 November LocalWP audit is reported delivered, with all 13 areas PARTIAL, no complete customer journey verified, two reported tool-output disclosure incidents and outstanding production inventories. B01 ingested the master evaluation and parent evidence baseline; B02 ingested PARENT_REVIEW_NOTES and REPORT_VALIDATION; B03 ingested AGENT_EXECUTION_LEDGER and COORDINATION_BRIEF; B04 ingested RF/PI, and B05 ingested CT/AR. B06 ingested CC/WC. B07 ingested MB/LM. Eight of thirteen detailed child snapshots ingested; five pending. All 13 child domains remain PARTIAL as of 19 Nov. This is dated evidence, not a fresh runtime audit. No current runtime inspection performed.

No bulk migration until per-capability foundation acceptance, mapping, reversible backup, bounded idempotent rehearsal, reconciliation and explicit approval. Do not restart R-0/R-1 wholesale; only targeted freshness/provenance gaps. Preserve native-first ownership and finish existing journeys before speculative custom systems. Existing Golden Journey work continues independently under its own authorization.

**B01 reconciliation outcome:** Native ownership remains coherent. Core installed file-to-journal alignment and ACF JSON parity are strong bounded evidence; Member Perks release provenance is unresolved. Four native Course/Product pairs now supersede the older zero-mapping observation; the only curriculum is on an unmapped synthetic Course. The representative purchase/access journey is not connected. Two reported disclosure incidents block the audit safety gate (not evidence of compromise). All acceptance flags remain scoped; no capability is migration-ready. No changes to business decisions, WordPress, GitHub or Plane.

**B02 reconciliation outcome:** Three supplied manifest-listed documents (ME, PB, PRN) independently match their recorded bytes, lines and SHA-256; RV is self-excluded. This verifies document identity, not underlying runtime truth. Parent evidence strengthens specific static checks and corrects interpretation, while all 13 domains remain PARTIAL. Newly explicit unsafe read paths: HappyFiles admin_init and term hooks can write, Tickera attendance getter normalizes and persists data, admin thank-you can complete an unrelated order, and Masteriyo send marker does not establish delivery. Member Perks scope-policy divergence is confirmed in compared code, not an active exploit. No approved business rule or acceptance stage changes. R-2 only.

**B03 reconciliation outcome:** Both supplied orchestration documents match the original manifest independently (5/18 cumulative). The audit ledger records 13 distinct child assignments and submitted reports, executed model evidence as reported, maximum-four concurrency and a parent-review gate before Agent 13. The brief establishes bounded read-only inspection, report structure, source/runtime evidence classes and non-mutation/retirement gates. These are documentary governance and reported orchestration, not a new website capability, raw execution replay or proof of universal compliance. The two audit safety incidents remain blocking. No business decisions, runtime state or acceptance stages change.

**B04 reconciliation outcome:** RF and PI match their original manifest identities (7/18). Two of thirteen domain snapshots now ingested; both remain PARTIAL. Dated runtime identity, actual PHP SAPI, socket, configuration, native provider inventory and exact PE-007/008 lifecycle are substantially better evidenced. Core 63-file journal parity is independently reproduced by historical parent; not live behaviour. Member Perks source drift, production-mode label on local, snippet index version and missing source lineage, plugin dependency/consumer uncertainty, PHP registry/DB transport discrepancies and stale deployment documentation remain open. No current runtime test or authority change.

**B05 reconciliation outcome:** CT/AR match original manifest (9/18); 4/13 detailed domain reports ingested. Dated effective 63 CPTs/55 taxonomies, 394 registered terms + 8 unregistered terms, 873 wp_posts rows (not HPOS/plugin tables); 28 ACF groups and 247 field definitions reported. **B10 reconciliation outcome:** RD matches the original manifest; all 18 manifest-listed files independently rechecked against available uploads, 13/13 individual reports plus all supporting documents (RV self-excluded). B01 distinguishes parent-reproduced evidence (reported by ME/PB) from delegated observations; neither is a fresh independent run by this reconciliation agent. Historical dated evidence can justify partial implementation classifications *at that baseline*, not current behaviour, loaded hooks or production acceptance.

Authority order is by question, not a single blanket hierarchy: owner-confirmed BIZ governs desired business rules; locked architecture and scoped AGENTS/contracts govern implementation; actual pinned source establishes code; timestamped observed configuration establishes runtime; controlled tests establish behaviour; Plane governs work/dependencies and recorded approvals; LEG governs retirement. Historical Drive proposals and child audit interpretations cannot override current decisions. Parent review corrections supersede disproven child interpretations only in demonstrated scope. Any business/architecture contradiction must be explicitly reconciled, not silently resolved. Evidence of absence requires bounded search; otherwise UNKNOWN.

**Native capability review:** WordPress → specialist plugin/configuration → ACF simple relationships/Options → Bricks Query Loop → supported APIs → minimal adapter → measured limitation → custom subsystem only with Master Gate approval. Root PHP minimum/CI 8.5; source-controlled definitions; Bricks/ACSS first; BEM and context labels; FluentSnippets only for ordinary snippets; Platform Core and Member Perks scoped plugin exceptions. Root runtime mutation coordination is mandatory for future authorised mutations. No runtime tools invoked.

## 4. Business requirements coverage matrix

### 4.1 Classifications and row contract

Implementation: `IMPLEMENTED_AND_VERIFIED`, `IMPLEMENTED_NOT_BEHAVIOR_VERIFIED`, `CONFIGURED`, `PARTIALLY_IMPLEMENTED`, `DOCUMENTED_ONLY`, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, `CONFLICT`, `UNKNOWN`, `NOT_APPLICABLE`.

Evidence: `SOURCE_CONFIRMED`, `RUNTIME_CONFIRMED`, `BOTH_CONFIRMED`, `HISTORICAL_ONLY`, `INFERRED`, `UNKNOWN`, `CONFLICT`. Evidence for written ownership is SOURCE_CONFIRMED; evidence for current implementation is independently UNKNOWN unless specifically shown. `DOCUMENTED_ONLY` is not evidence that code is absent. Owner-confirmed requirements have BUSINESS_APPROVED for business intent ONLY; future options and unresolved qualifications are not approved. Separate SOURCE_MERGED, RUNTIME_APPLIED, RUNTIME_LOADED, BEHAVIOR_VERIFIED, BUSINESS_APPROVED and MIGRATION_READY. All implementation proof flags are UNKNOWN / awaiting evidence unless explicitly identified; MIGRATION_READY = NOT ESTABLISHED. No row is currently accepted.

Each child row below inherits domain authority, known/intended consumers (actual wiring unverified), lifecycle obligations, Plane mapping and acceptance scenario from its domain header. Row-specific source wording preserves qualifiers; verification requires the exact rule, positive/negative cases and the applicable TV checks. Implementation/config/source-path/runtime timestamp fields default to UNKNOWN / awaiting evidence; evidence baseline for business intent is BIZ recorded dates, for written ownership ROOT 25 Nov; actual observed configuration and deployed source refs are now available for scoped B01 findings below. B01 classifications apply to the 19 Nov baseline, NOT today. All unmodified UNKNOWN rows retain their original meaning. Disposition INVESTIGATE unless stated; owner approval only as quoted. These IDs are trace keys local to this register, not newly approved business policies or Plane tasks. BIZ line anchors disambiguate reused IDs. Future batches replace UNKNOWN only with exact scoped evidence, retaining earlier observations and conflicts.

**B01 row notation:** `HISTORICAL_ONLY (underlying RUNTIME_CONFIRMED/SOURCE_CONFIRMED/BOTH_CONFIRMED)` indicates source type as reported in ME/PB, not a fresh current inspection. The stable E01–E63 evidence keys in §7 give exact line anchors, date, scope, limits, consumers, proposed disposition and next check. Rows updated below preserve exact BIZ language and owner intent. Full capability remains UNKNOWN where the documents only name an adjacent system. For all rows, BEHAVIOR_VERIFIED remains unestablished for current runtime; historical bounded VS1 behaviour is separately recorded. No owner policy was newly approved.

### 4.11 AU — Podcasts / media

**Source:** §26 A-01–A-05. **Authority:** WP/ACF/Bricks candidate presentation; PMPro access; host/feed/master custody unresolved. **Consumers/dependencies:** Mense, editorial, broadcasts, reader distinction, external providers. **Lifecycle obligations:** Series, episode/derivative, asset, licence, external distribution, promotion. **Plane mapping (from BIZ, not verified individually):** VGN-25, VGN-55/56. **Verification family:** TV-78–82. **Acceptance scenario (proposed):** Trace master/source to derivative, feed and on-site player; preserve rights, enforce configured access, handle unavailable media and withdrawal.

| Trace key / source | Exact recorded business rule / qualification | Owner intent | Implementation / verification / disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-AU-1700 · A-01 / F-103 (BIZ:L1700, §26) | Support **independent podcast series** with episodes and **podcast/audio editions derived from existing programme or other content**. Their identities and source relationships must be distinguishable so an audio edition does not automatically produce an unrelated duplicated canonical programme/episode. Exact editorial identifiers, URLs, source linkage and representation are not yet approved. | Confirmed as recorded; preserve qualification | UNKNOWN / UNKNOWN / INVESTIGATE B05: E54/E60: five broadcasts include two audio/podcast assignments; editorial_format Audio has zero edges. Separate podcast identity, rights/master/feed and access not established. B08: E100/E101: five uitsending records and divergent format/series generations; no independent podcast identity or derivation proof. B09: E108/E118: Voelgoed LIVE routes stored; no independent podcast model/provider evidence. B10: DC-16/17; E124: two Podcast assignments in existing format vocabulary; no separate CPT established, not grounds to reject independent/derived podcast capability. |
| R-AU-1701 · A-02 / F-104 (BIZ:L1701, §26) | Support **on-site podcast listening** and distribution to **Spotify, Apple Podcasts, YouTube and other relevant platforms**. No podcast host, feed manager, RSS publication mechanism, native player, service integration, auto-sync or existing platform connection was approved or runtime-verified here. Rights and individual platform constraints must be respected; do not assert every podcast is already distributed. | Confirmed as recorded; preserve qualification | UNKNOWN / UNKNOWN / INVESTIGATE B05: E54/E60: five broadcasts include two audio/podcast assignments; editorial_format Audio has zero edges. Separate podcast identity, rights/master/feed and access not established. B08: E101/E103: episode media/host/feed/player not inspected; local file presence not distribution proof. B09: E118: no external media/feed/player/host inspection. B10: DC-17; E124: preserve existing episodes and investigate feeds, on-site player and provider URLs. |
| R-AU-1702 · A-03 / F-105 (BIZ:L1702, §26) | Podcast listening/access is configurable **per podcast series or individual episode**. The exact choices (public, PMPro member-only, specific member tiers, purchase-only, embargo, teaser) and exception precedence need later approval. Do not infer that all podcasts are paid, all are free or that a customer who has an audiobook owns podcast access. Preserve S1-Q05: current Voelgoed LIVE episodes/highlights remain free/public until separately changed. | Confirmed as recorded; preserve qualification | UNKNOWN / UNKNOWN / INVESTIGATE B05: E54/E60: five broadcasts include two audio/podcast assignments; editorial_format Audio has zero edges. ### 4.12 ID — Identity / CRM / operations

### 5.3 Runtime snippet ownership and execution map (B04)

Historical direct registry/SELECT inventory, not fresh runtime. Effective flags are not actual role grants, REST exposure or successful routes. `wp_posts` totals exclude HPOS and other plugin tables. The four unregistered post keys total 21 rows; the three unregistered taxonomies total eight terms. Report 03 owner labels are tool metadata, not effective save authority. #### Effective registered taxonomies (CT §3.2)

Visibility columns are `H/P/PQ` = `hierarchical / public / publicly_queryable`; `UI/Menu/Nav/Tag/AdminCol/REST` = `show_ui / show_in_menu / show_in_nav_menus / show_tagcloud / show_admin_column / show_in_rest`. Capability values are the effective `manage_terms/edit_terms/delete_terms/assign_terms` strings.

#### Full term hierarchy and IDs (CT §3.3)

Each stored registered term is listed as `#term_id Name [slug; parent; stored_count]`. Parent `0` is a root; any other parent is the WordPress term ID. `stored_count` is the `term_taxonomy.count` field and can differ from the direct relationship count. Taxonomies with no stored terms are explicitly shown.

- `category` (1): #1 Voelgoed [slug=`voelgoed`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=0].
- `post_tag`: 0 stored terms.
- `nav_menu`: 0 stored terms.
- `link_category`: 0 stored terms.
- `post_format`: 0 stored terms.
- `wp_theme` (1): #53 bricks-child [slug=`bricks-child`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=1].
- `wp_template_part_area`: 0 stored terms.
- `wp_pattern_category`: 0 stored terms.
- `event_category` (8): #16 Aanlyn, Uitsendings … [slug=`aanlyn`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=2]; #19 Donasies, kompetisies … [slug=`kompetisies`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=1]; #14 Feeste, markte ... [slug=`feeste`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=2]; #12 Funksies, vroue-oggende … [slug=`funksies`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=2]; #17 Reise, toere … [slug=`reise`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=2]; #18 Sport, gholfdae ... [slug=`sport`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=1]; #13 Vertonings, produksies … [slug=`vertonings`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=1]; #15 Werkswinkels, praatjies … [slug=`werkswinkels`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=2].
- `course_cat`: 0 stored terms.
- `course_tag`: 0 stored terms.
- `course_difficulty` (3): #4 Advanced [slug=`advanced`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=0]; #2 Beginner [slug=`beginner`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=0]; #3 Intermediate [slug=`intermediate`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=0].
- `course_visibility` (1): #54 free [slug=`free`; parent=0 (root); stored_count=1].

B05 corrections to the above historical inventory: the single `content_topic` edge is synthetic Article 1887 (PRN:L105), not unexplained migration; no real editorial coverage. `kompetisie_entry` is the observed key, not approved renaming. `tc_api_keys` has 18 published records, no keys or customer information read; queryability warrants a safe permission assessment, not a vulnerability assertion. `event_category` has 13 assignments across 17 events; assignments are not distinct event coverage. `course_visibility` includes one published and one trashed edge, not proof of free entitlement. `shop_order`/`shop_subscription` zero post rows do not prove no HPOS records. HappyFiles `hf_cat_mentor` attaches to absent `mentor`; exact owner/use and lineage open. `vg_perk_runtime_status` taxonomy terms and assigned drafts are persisted projections, not authoritative live claim eligibility. Retain vendor and legacy registrations; map consumers and permissions before any changes.

### 5.5 ACF group, options-page and field schema register — B05 (AR:L48–581)

All schema defaults/choices below are definitions, not business-approved rules or actual stored values. Field/group/option IDs are schema identifiers. The seven root JSON groups, two Perks JSON groups, two PHP groups and 17 DB-classified groups are reported by ACF; one DB group inactive. Root JSON/selected schema parity does not imply all DB fields are portable. Group source labels are conflicting tool metadata, not proof of competing authorities. Perks nested owner controls its plugin source; no approval of current runtime release. Group dispositions: retain canonical native fields and plugin-owned fields; reconcile DB-only portability/source and runtime-only writers; investigate inactive promo and draft-only landing/testimonial consumers, no removal. Corrected counts and constraints below override original bounded report statements.

### Options pages

The four database-managed pages came from the ACF options-page list and full-page reads. The `vgmp-settings` page is registered in `voelgoed-member-perks/includes/Admin/SettingsPage.php:14-27`; it did not appear in the ACF list, and the full-page lookup did not resolve it. Its field-group JSON uses the `vgmp-settings` location. The plugin settings repository reads its values through the ACF-backed configuration provider, including option names referenced in `voelgoed-member-perks/includes/Config/SettingsRepository.php:29-108`.

| `uitsending` | Public, archive `/voelgoed-live/` | Voelgoed Live episodes | 5 published | ACF post-type list and aggregate count |

The ACF post-type list labels `person`, `event_participation`, and `product_contribution` as JSON source. The full post-type getter labels those same definitions as DB source. This is a `CONFLICT` in ability metadata. The source files under `site-code/acf-json` define the Person and relationship CPTs, and the runtime source checkout contains those files.

`tc_events` is owned by Tickera, `mto-course` by Masteriyo, and `product` by WooCommerce. They are not counted as ACF-registered post types.

Two further taxonomies are not ACF-managed. Platform Core registers public hierarchical `content_topic` and attaches it to posts, courses, products, events, current medarbeiders, broadcasts, competitions, and Person. It has 55 terms, seven roots, and one direct object edge. Tickera registers `event_category`; it has eight terms and 13 object edges. The two taxonomies have separate owners and separate jobs.

### Schema architecture and configuration (AR section 4)

### ACF ownership

The inspected inventory has 28 groups:

- 17 groups classified as database source by the ACF group list.
- 7 repo-owned JSON groups under `site-code/acf-json`, each also present in the runtime database.
- 2 plugin JSON groups under `voelgoed-member-perks/acf-json`.
- 2 Platform Core PHP groups.

The 247 field definitions comprise 134 fields in the 24 database-backed groups, 83 top-level Perks JSON fields, 28 nested Perks repeater fields, and 2 PHP-registered fields. The counts include tab fields. The field tables below include IDs where ACF exposes a database field ID, keys, names, labels, types, required flags, schema defaults, locations, conditional logic, return formats, relationship targets, and available bidirectional settings. For JSON-only and PHP-local fields without database child IDs, the ID is shown as `n/a`. “blank” means an explicit empty schema default; “n/a” means the schema has no default property. These are schema defaults, not current record values.

The seven repo JSON groups are Article People, Course People, Editorial Classification, Event Participation Details, Person Details, Person Related Content, and Product Contribution Details. The two plugin JSON groups are Voelgoed Perk Settings and Member Perk Details. The PHP groups are Content Topic Roles and Product People.

### Member Perks JSON field schemas

The two plugin files define 83 top-level fields, including tabs, plus 28 repeater children. The schema has no conditional logic on these fields. The ACF JSON definitions and runtime plugin copies have matching SHA-256 hashes. Repeater child rows are nested under their parent in the field table.

### 5.6 Aggregate field populations and consumer boundary — B05 (AR:L690–765)

Original aggregate table retained with explicit corrections, not to be interpreted as current data or all distinct real business entities.

### Stored relationship counts

All counts below are aggregate counts. No referenced record IDs or stored values are included.

| Relationship | Runtime aggregate | Integrity finding |
|---|---:|---|
| Article primary author | 0 owners, 0 references | No current forward edge |
| Article additional authors | 0 owners, 0 references | No current forward edge |
| Article expert contributors | 1 owner, 1 reference | Valid Person edge; reverse field matches |

| Person reverse Article fields | Expert field 1 owner / 1 reference; other fields empty | Expert edge matches forward edge |
| Course lead instructor | 0 owners, 0 references | No current forward edge |
| Course additional instructors | 1 owner, 1 reference | One valid Person edge |
| Course experts | 0 owners, 0 references | No current forward edge |

| Legacy Event Medewerkers | 2 event owners, 4 references | All references resolve to current `medewerker` records; no repeated ID within an event field |
| Legacy Product source event | 3 product owners, 3 references | All references resolve to current `tc_events` records |

The reverse Course reference is a stale edge relative to current post rows. Its source or delete history was not available. It may be old metadata from a removed or otherwise absent Course; that explanation is **UNKNOWN**. No duplicate forward Article, Course, Event, or Product relationship was found in the inspected aggregates.

### Field-group population

The numerator is the count of distinct ACF field names with at least one nonempty stored value. The denominator is the number of non-tab fields; nested Perks repeater fields are included in their denominators. This scan counted keys only and did not expose their values. A zero means no nonempty data was found for that group's fields, not that the group has no code consumer.

### Domain coverage

| Domain | Schema and consumers | Runtime evidence | Coverage |
|---|---|---|---|
| Article | Native ACF Person fields, reverse targets, topic classification, article lede/read time, FCM consumer | 2 published posts; one expert edge; one direct topic edge; no author, additional-author, or reviewer references | PARTIAL |
| Person | Canonical public `person`, Person Details, reverse article/course references, `person_type` | 1 published Person; two Person Type edges; no data in Person Details fields; one stale reverse Course reference | PARTIAL |
| Course | Three native Person relationships and reverse fields | 6 published Courses; one valid additional-instructor edge; one unmatched reverse reference | PARTIAL |
| Event | Tickera event, legacy staff, Event Participation, Event Landing, topic/taxonomy fields | 17 published events; 2 legacy staff owners and 4 references; no Event Participation records; Event Landing fields empty | PARTIAL |
| Product | WooCommerce product, legacy event/staff fields, Product Contribution, Product People mode | 8 published products plus other statuses; 3 source-event references and 3 legacy staff references; no Product Contribution records; mode empty | PARTIAL |
| Topic | Platform Core canonical tree and optional Primary role | 55 terms, 7 roots, 1 direct edge, 0 Primary values; `content_category` has 0 edges | PARTIAL |
| Member Perks | Plugin-owned settings and Perk schema, integrations read the fields | Nonempty schema data appears across settings and Perk records; no checkout/claim behavior was exercised | PARTIAL |

The content-category taxonomy is empty of current object edges, while the runtime-only FCM snippet selects featured fields by `content_category` slug. That leaves the observed FCM selector without a currently assigned content category. The runtime snippet maps the seven canonical semantic slugs and excludes `featured_leefstyl`; the schema includes the latter field. Because Leefstyl is a commercial classification, this is a consumer gap to review, not proof the field is redundant.

The Bricks scan found the Article, Person, Course, Event, Product, Medewerker, and Voelgoed Live templates and their configured fields or query markers. The Event Landing and Testimonial fields appear only in draft template 1919. The Calendar templates 137 and 280 contain `{acf_dorp}`, but no inspected ACF group defines a `dorp` field. Whether another provider or legacy group owns that tag is **UNKNOWN**.

**Controlling correction to original table:** Event staff 2 owners/4 references includes current Event 763 and revision 769; only one current Event with two profile links (PRN:L95). Excluded owner fixture edges (PRN:L105) must not be migrated. Home Featured Destination 1/1 is contradicted by direct page-specific parent evidence (PRN:L121): no value/marker on draft page 1926, optional slot not defect. The 452 owner objects in Perks population are aggregate scan scope, not 452 Perk records; actual observed Perks 15 published/5 draft. Broadcast 1/10 populated field names does not identify actual state, URLs or media rights. Zero field values or empty CPTs do not establish no consumer. `acf_dorp` unresolved. Source population tables remain dated; no personal values copied.

The manifest sets these snippets to published with conditions disabled; the JS body is assigned to `wp_footer`, and the PHP snippets use `all`. These are registry settings, not an observed callback list. Snippet 6’s initial backfill runs on any admin request reaching `admin_init` when its marker is absent; the function does not check an administrator capability before its bulk writes.

The MU plugin registers `template_redirect` priority 1, `pre_get_posts`, `woocommerce_product_query`, and `woocommerce_shortcode_products_query`. It reads `_masteriyo_course_id` and `_wc_product_id`, Masteriyo/Woo product types and course status; redirects a linked product single only when the linked course is published; excludes linked products from frontend catalog queries; and skips admin, AJAX, REST, cart, checkout, and account contexts. It uses `wp_safe_redirect`; it does not write records or register REST/AJAX endpoints. Presence in `mu-plugins/` implies WordPress’s automatic MU load path, but no request was made to prove execution.

### Bricks child theme behavior

The runtime `functions.php` registers `enqueue_mentor_animation_assets` on `wp_enqueue_scripts`, enqueues GSAP 3.12.5 and ScrollTrigger when in a legacy `mentor` context, any Bricks preview, or the Bricks builder; registers `elements/title.php` on `init`; filters builder labels, allowed HTML tags, and Bricks echo function names; updates `_cached_event_date_short` on `save_post_tc_events`; changes Tickera `menu_order` to the parsed event timestamp on the same save hook; and overrides admin menu order/styles (`functions.php:5–25,47–88,90–129,133–250`). The hooks read `event_date_time` through `get_post_meta`; the two save handlers write post meta and post `menu_order`. `style.css` contains only the child-theme header, with no custom CSS rules. No current event/order or template output was read.

`elements/title.php` defines the generic `Element_Custom_Title` Bricks element with title/subtitle controls and output (`elements/title.php:4–19,27–79,86–123`). It inserts configured values into markup without an explicit escaping call at the render site. No unsafe runtime output was reproduced; whether this element is used and which roles may edit its controls remain unknown. The theme echo allow-list names `format_tickera_date` with an inline comment that it does not exist, and also names `get_formatted_tickera_date` and `test_bricks_echo`; no definitions were found in the repository search. The legacy `mentor` string is an asset-loading condition only; this code does not register the prohibited `mentor` CPT or route.

Loader: CC:L179 identifies CodeHandler.php plugins_loaded priority 9; CodeRunner.php reads published/error index, attaches PHP at setup_theme if conditions disabled (wp if enabled), emits JS at wp_footer. Index error_files empty on inspection; force_disabled=no per PI, cached_version 10.56 vs manager10.58. Refresh/sync separate REST action, not a read. Nine bodies eligible, actual loaded callback list unknown. CC:L65 notes no bodies found under uploads; actual storage in wp-content/fluent-snippet-storage. Snippet 10 source body matched; eight unmatched in bounded repo scan. Snippet 7 retention filter only; no direct cleanup. Child-theme write paths and raw title rendering require guarded review, not assumed corruption/exploit. Retain GSAP/ScrollTrigger until template consumers known; ClassicMonks absence unresolved (CF-56).

**Additional exact adapter surfaces (CC:L79–109,136–162):** Journey Intent user meta `_vgpc_journey_intent_v1`, cookie `vgpc_journey_intent_v1`, TTL filter `vgpc_journey_intent_ttl_days`, Bricks action `vgpc_journey_intent`, page933/form `jrn905` focus. FCM fields `featured_nuwe_jy`, `featured_geloof`, `featured_self`, `featured_werk_geld`, `featured_ouerskap`, `featured_huwelik`, `featured_mensstories` on `featured-content-manager`; first term by term_order. Tags `{vg_article_lede}`, `{vg_featured_content_id}`, `{vg_has_featured_content}`, `{vg_featured_content_type}`, `{vg_featured_object_type_label}`, `{vg_featured_object_cta_label}`, `{vg_article_read_time}`, legacy aliases. DashboardDynamicData init/render hooks and DashboardQueryProvider query/cache hooks scoped to context. Event query exact class `vg-event-participation-query` and section `vg-event-participants-section`; Product queries `vg-product-people-query`, `vg-person-products-query`, `vg-product-source-event-query`, matching empty-section controls. Perks source REST `/vg-member-perks/v1/claim`, current-user query, `/course-claim`, `/ticket-claim`; 12 source vgmp_* tags; `[vgmp_member_hub]`; source hooks rest_api_init, Bricks init/query/cache/tag filters, Woo before_calculate_totals/coupon change/cart-order-item meta/checkout validation/order processing/payment completion/status actions, wp_enqueue_scripts. Client user/status/amount/order fields not trusted in inspected source; current runtime behavior not inferred. The MU adapter uses template_redirect priority1, pre_get_posts, woocommerce_product_query, woocommerce_shortcode_products_query; static request cache, skips admin/AJAX/REST/cart/checkout/account, reads reciprocal links and product/course state; parent harness qualifies its intended single redirect. No custom controller for Event/Product adapters. All retained pending verified consumer and source authority.

**Counts:** 24 parent products: 13 simple, 7 variable-subscription, and 4 `mto_course`; 8 published, 7 draft, 5 pending, 1 private, 3 trash. Fourteen child variations are published. The seven parent subscription products are draft even though their variations are published; parent draft status means those variations are not evidence of a public offer. No product-level demo/test flag was found among the inspected markers; the table labels test/QA items using titles and the Tickera/course links. No product tags, additional product-category assignments, or other product attributes were observed. The only configured product category on records with terms is `uncategorized`; subscription parents define the one local custom attribute `Betalingstermyn`.

All priced products were in stock according to stored `_stock_status`; individual product stock management was off. The global stock-management option is on, with a 10,080-minute hold-stock value. The four course products are virtual/downloadable. The test event and ordinary physical products are virtual=no. The subscription parent records are virtual=no while each subscription variation is virtual=yes. No shipping class was assigned in the selected product records.

The course data consists of six published records (five ordinary IDs plus synthetic Course 1889) and one trashed course. Product 1935’s configured permalink is `http://localhost:10039/winkel/mindfulness-vir-vroue/`; the MU-plugin redirects a linked published product single to the course permalink and excludes linked course products from Woo catalogue queries (runtime file lines 130–185). That code is SOURCE_CONFIRMED at runtime, but is absent from the checked-out repository file list and its current route was not requested.

Tickera has 17 published event records in the selected query. Eight configured event-category terms map as follows: Event 151 `aanlyn`, 163 `kompetisies`, 164 `feeste`, 165 `funksies`, 166 `sport`, 167 `vertonings`, 172 `reise`, and 181 `werkswinkels`. Other published event rows observed were 542 (`funksies`), 545 (`werkswinkels`), 548 (`feeste`), 551 (`aanlyn`), 554 (`reise`), and test/QA events 763, 1189, 1191, 1193 (no category assigned). Four Woo test products 842, 844, 845, 846 all carry `_tc_is_ticket=yes` and `_event_name=763`. Separate Tickera-native test tickets 1195, 1196 and 1197 map to events 1189, 1191 and 1193; those Tickera ticket records have no Woo price. No attendee, order or ticket-fulfillment data was inspected.

## Payment gateway configuration vs guest availability

| Gateway / provider | Configured state | Guest availability / behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Woo Order Test (`wpfi_woo_order_test`) | Active plugin; Woo setting enabled=yes; `admin_payment_bypass_enabled=yes` | VGN-18 latest browser comment says guest checkout had no available methods. Installed `process_payment()` calls `payment_complete()` only for administrators and shows an error for non-admins (`woo-order-test/admin/functions.php:152–163`). Therefore enabled configuration is not evidence of a usable guest payment method. |
| Cash on delivery | Woo gateway option enabled=no | Not available by config; no checkout call made |
| PayPal | Woo gateway option enabled=no | Not available by config; no checkout call made |
| PMPro Paystack | PMPro’s selected gateway option is `paystack` | PMPro provider selection is not a configured WooCommerce gateway; no PMPro payment attempted |
| PMPro PayFast | PMPro PayFast extension active but PMPro currently selects Paystack | PMPro-only extension, not an enabled Woo gateway; not used in checkout |
| Any other Woo payment provider | No other enabled provider found in the selected gateway options | Unknown whether credentials/plugin activation or provider-specific settings exist outside selected options; no values inspected |

The test gateway’s administrator bypass filter can remove all checkout gateways for administrators and make payment unnecessary. Its thank-you callback, however, marks **any order whose thank-you route is reached by an administrator** completed; the callback does not check the order’s gateway (`woo-order-test/admin/functions.php:209–254`). This is a static code-path risk, not evidence that any order was changed. No orders were read. The test plugin’s activation hook defaults the administrator bypass option to yes (`woo-order-test/wc-ot.php:76`), consistent with the current selected value.

**Controlling qualifications:** E16/PRN: MU catalog exclusion may precede redirect (parent isolated PHP harness only); current 404 unknown. Test gateway thank-you callback lacks payment method, current status and bypass-option guards; no order touched. Guest payment methods unavailable 17 Nov, not current render. PMPro Paystack selection not Woo gateway. Seven subscription parents draft; no active subscription records inspected. Product visibility test/QA markers not complete classification; no live customer exposure established. 17 event records enumerated, 13 event-category edges, no attendee evidence in this report. Fixture Event763 and product edges remain excluded by owner from automatic migration. All product prices and option values require owner approval before live commercial use.

### 5.10 Membership programme, entitlement and runtime custody inventory — B07 (MB)

All observations below date to 19 Nov 2026. Existing §5.9 exact price inventory remains authoritative for that snapshot, augmented by group/mapping data here. No member, subscription, redemption or order records inspected by MB. PMPro Woo 1.11 bridges WCS/Woo to PMPro 3.9.1; Woo 11.3.0/WCS 9.4.1 own commercial state. Member Perks 0.18.0 owns benefit configuration and claim ledger, Masteriyo 3.5.1 owns course access. Retain all providers; no new engine.

| Programme | Tier / PMPro level | Group | Woo parent | Monthly variation: regular → sale/current | Yearly variation: regular → sale/current |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Vriendinneklub | Brons / 15 | 4 | 905 | 906: R39 → R24 | 907: R390 → R240 |
| Vriendinneklub | Silwer / 16 | 4 | 908 | 909: R59 → R39 | 910: R590 → R390 |
| Vriendinneklub | Goud / 17 | 4 | 911 | 912: R99 → R79 | 913: R990 → R790 |

| Voelgoednetwerk | Platinum / 21 | 5 | 923 | 924: R444 → R344 | 925: R4,440 → R3,440 |

Current PMPro configuration confirms seven levels with `allow_signups=0`, `initial_payment=0`, `billing_amount=0`, no cycle period/number, no billing limit, no trial, and no expiry. Levels 15–17 are grouped in “Voelgoedvriendinne” group 4; levels 18–21 are grouped in “Voelgoednetwerk” group 5. Both groups have `allow_multiple_selections=0`. Each PMPro level’s `vg_membership_code`, programme key, level key and labels match the Member Perks mappings. Current mappings have both programmes and all seven levels enabled. The Member Perks programme `upsell_page_id` values are blank.

PMPro levels have no cycle/limit/trial/expiry, zero initial/billing amounts and direct signup disabled; both groups disallow multiple selections *within* a group. Matching `vg_membership_code`, programme/level keys and labels, both programmes and all seven mappings enabled; no upsell page IDs. `_membership_product_autocomplete=0` each. Parent drafts deliberate until commercialization approval; do not infer free membership or public purchase.

| Setting | Current value | Meaning and limit |
|---|---:|---|
| `vgmp_environment_mode` | `production` | Label does not match the LocalWP staging purpose; code effect not established |
| `vgmp_enable_hub_display` | `1` | Hub display config enabled; page rendering not tested |
| `vgmp_enable_runtime_status_cron` | `1` | Scheduling config enabled; execution not observed |
| `vgmp_enable_woo_discounts` | `0` | Member Perks Woo discount execution disabled |
| `vgmp_allow_initial_subscription_product_discounts` | `0` | Initial subscription product discount path disabled |
| `vgmp_enable_course_claims` | `0` | Masteriyo claim/enrolment path disabled |
| `vgmp_enable_ticket_claims` | `0` | Ticket claim path disabled |
| `vgmp_enable_woo_blocks_compat` | `0` | Woo Blocks compatibility path disabled |
| `vgmp_enable_notifications` | `0` | Member Perks notification path disabled |

The two configured programmes are `vriendinneklub` → page 991 and `voelgoednetwerk` → page 992. The hub service checks login and then the current PMPro profile. It returns `logged_out`, `dependency_missing`, `no_membership`, `programme_not_active`, or `can_view`; mapping errors fail closed. The shortcode renders an empty-state message when the visitor is logged out or not eligible. Current data does not supply a programme upsell page.

There are 15 published and five draft `vg_perk` records. The published set contains ten `course/free` entries, two product entries (one 20% product perk and one free product perk), one page/free entry, and two incomplete entries. Four draft entries are product percentage discounts and one is a free Woo-product-target entry. Published course perks target courses 532, 534, 536, 538 and 540; no published course perk targets Course 1889. These are staging records, some clearly QA-shaped by their slugs/configuration. They do not establish approved member offers. The current feature flags prevent treating them as active course/discount entitlements.

Published course Perk targets two per course 532/534/536/538/540, none for QA1889 (LM corroborates). No approved matrix, claim history, active user eligibility or actual grant. Ten course/free, two product, one page/free, two incomplete published; draft four percentage product plus one free Woo target. Preserve fixtures/records until classification and rights/consumer review.

The eight configured category terms and published-event counts are `funksies` (2), `vertonings` (1), `feeste` (2), `werkswinkels` (2), `aanlyn` (2), `reise` (2), `sport` (1), and `kompetisies` (1). The 13 assignments leave four events without a category: 763, 1189, 1191 and 1193.

**Event and classification authority.** Tickera registers public `tc_events` and the hierarchical `event_category` taxonomy. Current event records use Tickera metadata `event_date_time`, `event_end_date_time` and `event_location`; dates exist for all 17 published events and the location key exists on 14, including empty values. Event categories are separate from ticket types. The project should continue to treat Tickera's event/category model as authoritative.

**Ticket type and price mapping.** The three native `tc_tickets` records are all published, free (`price_per_ticket=0`) and configured with quantity 10:

| Ticket ID | Title | Event ID | Price / quantity | Issued instances |
|---:|---|---:|---|---:|
| 1195 | [QA] Early Ticket | 1189 | 0 / 10 | 2 under `order_paid` Tickera orders |
| 1196 | [QA] Late Ticket | 1191 | 0 / 10 | 1 under `order_paid` Tickera orders |
| 1197 | [QA] Past Ticket | 1193 | 0 / 10 | 1 under `order_paid` Tickera orders |

The four published Woo products are all flagged `_tc_is_ticket=yes`, map `_event_name=763`, have current price R999 and regular price R9,999, and are marked `instock`. Product 842 has `tc_ticket_id=842`; products 844, 845 and 846 have no `tc_ticket_id` value. All four select Tickera `tc_templates` post 41 ("Default"). No attendee or order data was used to produce the following aggregate:

| Product ID / title | Woo line-item quantity by status | Published attendee instances by parent order status |
|---|---|---|
| 842 / Event Test | completed 2; processing 1; pending 1 | completed 2; processing 1; pending 1 (4 total) |
| 844 / Event Test 2 | none | none |
| 845 / Event Test 3 | completed 1; processing 3 | completed 1; processing 3 (4 total) |
| 846 / Event Test 4 | cancelled 1; completed 1; on-hold 2; pending 5; processing 4 | completed 1; processing 4 (5 total) |

WooCommerce's current currency option is `ZAR`. The line-item totals and instance counts are present-state aggregates; they do not prove checkout, payment, cancellation or recovery events occurred in a particular sequence.

**Attendee and scanner state.** Each `tc_tickets_instances` record stores Tickera ticket/event linkage and order parentage. Four native instances have parent type `tc_orders` and Tickera status `order_paid`. Thirteen Woo-linked instances have parent type `shop_order_placehold`; their Woo order state is in HPOS. These Woo placeholder posts have WordPress status `draft`, while the HPOS orders have completed, processing or pending statuses. `tc_checkins` and `tc_checkouts` are absent from all 17 instances. No ticket codes or attendee fields are disclosed here.

The Tickera general option has `hide_checkin_ineligible_tickets=no` and `ean_13_checker=no`. The former affects the Attendees & Tickets admin listing. It is not a substitute for the bridge's scanner payment check. No Tickera `tickera_email_setting` option row was present. When that option is absent, bridge code defaults customer ticket-table flags to `yes`; PDF attachments require explicit per-email `woo_{email_id}_attach_ticket=yes`. No email or attachment was generated.

**People at events.** ACF group `Event Medewerkers` (group 748 per report 04) owns legacy field `event_medewerkers` on `tc_events`, linking to `medewerker`. Event 763 has profile IDs 753 and 757, both published `medewerker` posts. A second matching metadata row belongs to revision 769 of event 763, not a second event. The canonical ACF group `Event Participation Details` stores one Tickera event, one `person`, zero or more `event_role` terms, and non-negative display order. The four role terms are `spreker`, `paneellid`, `fasiliteerder` and `gas`; all currently have zero assignments. No canonical participation posts exist.

**Bricks templates.** Published Event Single 774 has the `tc_events` placement condition. Its people query is `event_participation`; its product loop filters `_event_name={post_id}`. Draft Event Landing 1919 has no placement conditions and previews event 542. It has 69 Bricks elements and seven named content sections in the stored design. Its ticket-products loop selects published Woo `product` records by `_event_name={post_id}`, without an `_tc_is_ticket=yes` clause. Its people loop selects `event_participation`; its testimonial loop names the query object `acf_event_landing_testimonials`. The active ACF group 1913 contains five optional fields: testimonials, gallery, video URL, programme and info image. No values were found on the 17 published events.

**E43 configured FPM module inventory (RF:L116; reported, not rerun):** The Local PHP-FPM binary was queried in info mode with the exact site ini and its bundled `bin/linux/shared-libs` library path. It reported these PHP modules: `bcmath`, `bz2`, `calendar`, `cgi-fcgi`, `Core`, `ctype`, `curl`, `date`, `dom`, `exif`, `FFI`, `fileinfo`, `filter`, `ftp`, `gd`, `gettext`, `hash`, `iconv`, `imagick`, `intl`, `json`, `lexbor`, `libxml`, `mbstring`, `mysqli`, `mysqlnd`, `openssl`, `pcntl`, `pcre`, `PDO`, `pdo_mysql`, `pdo_sqlite`, `Phar`, `posix`, `random`, `readline`, `Reflection`, `session`, `shmop`, `SimpleXML`, `soap`, `sockets`, `sodium`, `SPL`, `sqlite3`, `standard`, `sysvmsg`, `sysvsem`, `sysvshm`, `tidy`, `tokenizer`, `uri`, `xdebug`, `xml`, `xmlreader`, `xmlwriter`, `xsl`, `Zend OPcache`, `zip`, and `zlib`. Zend modules were `Xdebug` and `Zend OPcache`. The active FPM process map independently showed `imagick.so` and `xdebug.so`. The Local package library path was needed because the system loader otherwise could not find its bundled `libtidy` dependency.

## 12. Lifecycle contract register

All listed contracts are AWAITING APPROVED CONTRACT unless already approved exact source is ingested. Do not implement candidate enum labels. For every row complete explicit states and terminal/recovery states; transition table (from/to, trigger/actor, preconditions, guards, invariants, side effects); effective time; idempotency/concurrency; audit; failure/compensation; positive/negative tests. Existing provider mappings must be inspected and reused. Owner-approved business rules constrain the contract, not necessarily state names.

| LC | Concept group | Known business constraints / missing transitions and guards | Evidence / TV | B01 scoped obligations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LC-01 | Account, claim/link, session, lock, merge/unmerge | Verified ownership, no email-only merge; conflict/recovery/unlink/disable policy | I-01/02; TV-83 | E11/E15: signed guest cookie/user meta; login continuity bounded, cross-user isolation and verified link/recovery open. |
| LC-02 | Order/payment, line fulfilment, shipment, refund | Compatible mixed cart and identity; release only after authoritative eligibility; partial reversals and retries; terminal/refund rules open | C-01–05; TV-52–55 | E08/E16: admin thank-you mutation risk; guard gateway/payment, authorised actors, idempotent line fulfilment and compensation. B02: E29: admin actor is not sufficient payment authority; guard gateway/status/bypass, terminal/refund/duplicate handling and recovery. |

LC-03 includes claim scope and validator ownership. LC-17 includes lock failure/contended acquisition and request-level invalidation; no failure injection. All existing LC-01–19 remain.

**LC-22 — Audit evidence and review lifecycle (documentary governance, not application feature):** CB/AEL prescribe assignment → dispatch → bounded inspection → report saved → separate completion notification → parent complete read/qualification → initial 01–12 gate → 13 synthesis → final document validation. These are described steps, not approved application enums. Actors: owner authorizes scope, parent dispatches/reviews and handles tracker, each child writes only its own report; no subdelegation. Guards: exact target/ref, permitted safe read, maximum four active, completion notification before slot reuse, initial completeness/review before Agent 13; use advisory flock for expensive read batches. Side effects restricted to assigned Markdown and non-runtime read coordination; incidental runtime effects remain subject to assessment. Record unique task/report identity, preserve originals and explicit errata; retry/duplicate-notification, missing/failed report, cancellation, lock contention/timeout and recovery policy not fully specified by AEL/CB; no invented approved states. Delivery complete is not terminal platform acceptance; safety failure blocks audit acceptance until owner-controlled containment/correction and appropriate fresh evidence. No new task orchestration implementation authorized.

PE-008 prepared→stage_verified→swap_intent→swapped→health_verified→parity_verified→finalize_intent→finalized; rollback_intent→rolled_back or rollback_failed→guarded recovery. Finalized/rolled_back terminal record except explicitly guarded rollback. Actor coordinator and authorized operator/deployer; guards exact target/root/socket transport, owner one-use source/artifact, manifest/before-image, no unresolved journal, active session proof; side effects stage/atomic swap/journal/retained rollback. Concurrency exclusion and journal identity documented; replay/idempotency and crash/failure semantics on exact MySQL still require applicable contract verification; no failure injection. Free status is dated only. No recovery needed at observed baseline. No new states invented.

### B10 lifecycle refinement — LC-45 decommission evidence and reversible cutover (E128; DC-01–42; existing LC-15/17/23/35/38/40–44)

Proposed gate sequence, **not approved LEG state names or runtime transitions**: identify candidate → inventory read/write consumers across source/runtime/all statuses/external callers → establish approved replacement or retain → owner reviews scope, lineage, rights, excluded fixtures, retention and dependencies → separately authorize bounded rehearsal with verified backup and rollback → verify representative parity, side effects, security, accessibility and source/target reconciliation → separately authorize scoped cutover → verify remaining consumers and retention; only then consider retirement/archive under exact LEG authority. Actors: domain owner, source/operations/security owner where relevant, independent reviewer, authorized executor, Master Gate. Guard against duplicate or racing migrations/queue work using stable source identities, repeatable mappings, explicit execution IDs and existing PE-007/008 where applicable; actual implementation/idempotency/failure-injection proof pending. No concurrent writers or deletion based on a scan; define atomicity/checkpoint, partial failure compensation, retry/replay, cross-domain liability reconciliation and alert ownership. Possible outcomes retain/defer, rejected or verified retirement/archived document; approved terminal states and reopen/restore rules remain to be defined under LEG. Failed verification blocks forward transition and retains or restores approved before-image, old routes/metadata/consumers, evidence and rollback assets. Never restore revoked credentials. No new state, expiry, migration or archive rule adopted. Existing LC-20 marker and LC-25 reverse integrity, LC-31 paid-through, LC-36 ticket liabilities, LC-43 notification retry, LC-44 security incident controls remain separate gates.

## 13. Security, integrity and operational risks

P0: identity mislink or data exposure; entitlement/financial mismatch; double billing/issuance; unsafe ticket offline reconciliation; role/organiser scope leakage; two reported audit tool-output disclosure incidents awaiting separate safety/containment review (no raw data reproduced); lack of current restore/ingress/secret-handling proof. P1: public profile consent, paid media exposure, vote fraud, commercial editorial contamination, legacy consumers, rights/licensing. P2: frontend performance/accessibility and analytics polish, not a reason to skip acceptance. These are risk priorities, not assertions of actual security breaches or present failures. Do not inspect secret-bearing logs directly; request redacted metadata and separate containment evidence. Avoid production mutation, test transactions or clone creation without separate authority. LocalWP connector availability not established; production tools cannot substitute for local proof.

B01 risk prioritisation refinement: original B00 P0 labels describe potential impact families, not confirmed incidents. ME explicitly establishes **no confirmed live P0 data-loss/paid-access/payment-integrity incident**. Keep high-consequence unknowns open, but triage observed issues at P1/P2 per ME §J, not falsely report a breach.

### 14.3 Retirement readiness rule (LC-45; P56/LEG)

All candidates remain retained pending evidence unless already approved under their own narrowly bounded governance; even documentation-only archive requires owner confirmation and preservation of historical decision. No aggregate count, inactive field, zero assignment, source-only test, unassigned template or matching slug establishes safe removal. No new subsystem, plugin deactivation or third People/classification/relationship engine justified by RD. Existing BIZ capabilities remain requirements even where no native or external implementation has been evidenced. Full consumer and writer inventory, data lineage and retention, tested native/approved replacement, reversible before-image/restore, exact source/release and authority checks, owner decision and separate mutation authorization are mandatory before a specific decommission action. Preserve old routes/edges/history until cutover reconciliation passes.
